The gate at the head, four rulings in one batch — your "Four on the drawn map", "Road home", "best solution no corner cutting", "reason best solution for gameplay"; the plan approved in Plan Mode; five rules behind their own seams, ONE sweep with six arms on three surfaces, ONE commit.

Who speaks for Germany?" (the log census 182 → 183); the Balance tab's Questions panel; the guarantee rung's Question arm; the store inside `world.moments` (the affairs precedent).

> - **The crowns — "best solution no corner cutting" (DY-1f):** the levers never bit (no court held its grounds two turns); the road is the Emperor's gift — the ally petition asks for its AUTHORED design's provinces (Saxony, Hesse and Denmark could not be asked for before) and its prevalidation reads the validator's own occupied-homeland rule, so Pressburg's Tyrol reaches Bavaria (pinned end to end to the `rank_promotion` row); an AI aim peace signs for its allies' delivered aims ("Russia takes Finland. > - **The measure:** arm 0 reproduces session A byte for byte on both surfaces; **the Questions alone move all ten ambient seeds (on seven only the new column and the turn Prussia first reaches `fight` — the envy hastens a climb already under way), story classes 10 of 10 on every arm**; the tables turn 26 times — the German Question for Austria at turn 2 on every seed, the Italian on every seed, once for Prussia; **no second great-power war appears — said plainly:** the war column stands where session A left it on eight seeds, rivoli gains the Hanover war where the honour war stood, ulm calms; the reason is the board — the tables turn for Austria, at war already, and the only envious design with an army is Prussia's (the content levers put to you at session C's head); Bern's road clears the annexation line (1 → 0, Switzerland lives on ulm); **no crown taken** (the one the first run saw came with a defect); the band 28 / 27 / 26 on every arm; **the standing series does not move** (six arms, each seam's reach counted). > **▶ ▶ ▶ THE HOOK — THE ENGINE LANE SPLIT IN THREE — ✅ LANDED November 10, 2026: 10:48 → 8:49** (your "the hook takes so long is it valuable", "yea", "Yes, split it"; `tests/conftest.py` ENGINE_LANES and HEAVIEST_FIRST; `scripts/git-hooks/pre-commit`; pins `tests/test_the_engine_lanes.py`, 21):
> - **What the timed run read:** the hook's 10:48 was one lane. Every module that launches Godot shared ONE group, so one worker ran 31 modules in order for 10.0 minutes while seven sat idle.
> - **What landed:** the lane is three — `engine` (a real window or a sandboxed backend: one at a time, always; a census pins that a new such module joins it) and two lanes of headless harnesses beside it. The lane's floor 10.0 → 3.7 minutes. The heaviest groups are handed out first. The hook runs 10 workers. Two lane-only runs, a 12-worker and a 10-worker full run and the hook's own run: green, the same 27 skips (the 12-worker run's only two reds were my runner's own shell variable, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`).
> - **Found on the way:** `dd_b_play.py start` refuses while ANY Godot runs and the client-hands pins SKIP on the same test — while eight modules outside the lane had always run headless engines beside it. No run on record skipped them (27 skips, four runs), but only by timing. The suite now passes `--beside-headless` and counts windows only; a measure keeps the strict rail.
> - **The honest figure:** 8:49, not the 5–6 minutes I estimated. With the lane out of the way the suite is bound by its total work — about 73 minutes of summed test time — on eight cores and 16 GB: 12 workers measured 8:21 with free memory down to 0.2 GB, so the hook stays at 10 (1.2 GB free at the lowest). Ten groups hold 35% of the work (`test_cx_r2…` is one five-minute test, `test_iq7_review_round` five minutes more) — the next lever if you want the wait shorter: make those cheaper, no test removed.
> - **Next:** unchanged — session A (`docs/NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md`).

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE CONCERT — SESSION A: TIER 4 CLOSES — T4-2b THE COURT REMOVES + T4-3 PROMOTION — ✅ LANDED November 10, 2026 — TIER 4 COMPLETE** (`DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §2b session A, §3c "The seat" and G6 with their landing notes, §8 rows DY-1b and DY-1d; the rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §201; pins `tests/test_dy1_the_alarm_of_europe.py`, 55 new; the seams `a_court_removes_its_seat` (row T4-2b) and `a_minor_may_rise` (row T4-3); one commit, one measure sweep with three arms):
> - **The seat** (your "use best judgment but focus on utmost quality"): a court that signs its capital away to another AI court moves its seat to the richest homeland province it still holds, and comes home when the capital does — "The court of Bavaria removes to Franconia — Munich is Austria's by treaty." The new seat is a capital in every sense: guarded, counted in the war score, and a lawful object for the court's enemies. Nothing new is saved: the seat is read off the map's own capital flag.
> - **Two boundaries I drew, each one line to reverse:** your own court never removes (Paris signed away stays Paris lost); and a court whose capital YOUR house holds by treaty does not remove either — the Congress's recognition by defeat reads that capital in your hands, so the court stays under the knife as it does today. The rule binds between the AI courts.
> - **Promotion** (ruled at the gate): a court's rank is now derived from a law in force. Five minor courts carry one, the crowns of 1806 — Bavaria's Royal Crown (the Tyrol), Saxony's Royal Dignity (Posen), Hesse's Grand Ducal Title (Westphalia), Denmark's Both Shores of the Sound (Scania), Sardinia's Mainland Restored (Piedmont and Savoy); 3,000 gold, 100 a turn. A court may take its crown once it has held its grounds six turns; its force limit (15,000 → 30,000), its capital's guard (10,000 → 15,000) and its weight in a bloc and at the table rise at once, and it can no longer be made a vassal. Lose the grounds and the crown lapses.
> - **The measure:** with both switches down, the T4-2 records reproduce byte for byte on both surfaces. The seat alone moves all ten seeds: story classes 9 → 10; Bavaria's court sits in Franconia on every seed and Hanover's in Brunswick on six. With a seat to attack, Bavaria is attacked there, and Prussia keeps its word for her on seven seeds — it had never once been called on.
> - **What you asked to know — do the crowns bring the variety back? No.** No court took a crown on any of the ten seeds, on either surface; the run with the crowns is identical to the run without them. The nearest was Bavaria on friedland: the Tyrol taken at turn 34, lost at 36. The fallen courts, per seed: the Kingdom of Italy alone on nine; on ulm the Kingdom of Italy (turn 10) and Switzerland (turn 14) — and that one came from the seat, not the crowns.
> - **One province for you on the deploy gate's annexation line:** Bern, kept by Bavaria on ulm. Switzerland had gone over to Britain, attacked Bavaria's new seat and lost its last province; a client that falls signs nothing (T4-2's own exemption), so nobody hands Bern back. The AI-France harness reads 0. Yours to rule: leave it, or have a fallen client's last province handed back as a sovereign court's is.
> - **The difficulty guard holds:** France ends the three commanded campaigns at 28 / 27 / 26 provinces (28 / 25 / 25 with the switches down) — inside 23–28. The standing series did not move.
> - **Found and fixed on the way:** a peace that took a capital no longer counted as punitive once the court had removed (Prussia's Diktat on Hanover left no record) — the grievance now reads the capital that was lost, the seat where the court sits; the first measure sat Hanover's court in its poorest province because a cession is applied before the same treaty's evacuations — the richest province is now read once the peace has settled; the enemy AI read a signed-away capital as "lost" for ever; the bare scenario file refused the armyless minors' decks.
> - **The hook's figure:** 8:22 in the banner and CLAUDE.md (the run at f0b048d2, after the turn-6 board of one heavy module was restored once instead of 300 times).
> - **Next:** session B — Tier 5 whole, the Questions of Europe (`docs/NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md`). It opens with your rulings: the four Questions and their provinces (five of the drafted names are not on the map), where the latch is kept, and whether the crowns should be easier to reach.

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE CONCERT — T4-2 SIGN AT THE LAST — ✅ LANDED November 9, 2026; THE REMAINING PLAN RE-CUT INTO SEVEN SESSIONS** (row DY-1c, `DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §3c G5 and its landing note; the session plan §2b, by your "restructure remaining plan so we can do it in less sessions"; the rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §200; pins `tests/test_dy1_the_alarm_of_europe.py`, 28 new; the seam `a_crown_falls_only_by_treaty`, row T4-2):

The Kingdom of Italy still falls; so does any client, and any court whose homeland only your house holds.

> - **The measure:** the control reproduces T4-1 byte for byte. **The deploy gate's annexation line reads 0 on both surfaces** (7 → 0 on the ambient seeds, 7 → 0 for the AI courts of the AI-France harness) — nothing left to put to you province by province. T4-3 and Tier 5 are where variety is meant to come back; yours to say if Tier 4 should answer it instead.
> - **Found and fixed on the way:** the reckless auto-charge could take a court's last province without ever asking the erasure question, leaving a court alive with no land — it asks now; an AI capturer no longer plunders a province the peace handed home; a conqueror with no aim on its victim hands everything back (Prussia and Hesse) — as ruled, stated because it shows. The standing series re-pinned with its cause (France's bloc keeps Bavaria's weight); the suite's eight failures were all pins of the old world-lines, each re-seated with its cause.
> - **The seat** (your "use best judgment but focus on utmost quality"): a court whose capital is signed away will move its seat — its own rule, T4-2b, in session A.
> - **The plan re-cut** (`DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §2b): seven sessions to the deploy where the count read twelve to thirteen — A Tier 4 closes (the seat + promotion), B Tier 5 whole, C and D the Awakening, E and F Crowns & Recognition, G the deploy. No content cut; each rule keeps its own switch and its own arm of ONE measure; one commit and one review a session; your rulings asked once, at each session's head. Two places it may take one more session are named there (the Questions panel; the guided crown UI).
> - **Next:** session A (`docs/NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md`).

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE CONCERT — TIER 4'S GATE RULED (§3c) + T4-1 THE ALARM OF EUROPE — ✅ LANDED November 9, 2026** (`DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §3c, ruled in Plan Mode at the tier's head — your "your call" on the erasure question, Q5 and Q6; §8 rows DY-1 ✅ and DY-1c new; the rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §199; pins `tests/test_dy1_the_alarm_of_europe.py`, 53; the three seams in `tests/_t3_seams.py` row T4-1, the recorded boards holding them down):
> - **What the survey found:** every conqueror already paid threat at capture, AI courts included — but nothing ever acted on a non-French court's threat short of the hegemon's league; and no guarantee can save Bavaria in 1805, because she marched beside France and a guarantee only defends. So Franconia (the T3-6 residual) needs a rule about erasure itself.
> - **Your three questions, my calls:** erasure → **sign at the last** (a court whose last province falls signs a peace and keeps the homeland its conqueror may not keep, instead of vanishing — row DY-1c, T4-2); promotion → teeth at once (T4-3); alarm → words only.
> - **The Alarm:** an AI court's capture alarms Europe against it (6 a province, 12 a capital, 8 a third court's client; your captures add nothing new); an alarmed neighbour gives its word for the next victim, and the guarantee machinery does the rest; Talleyrand, the Balance tab and Le Moniteur say it in sentences ("Vienna's appetite is remarked in Berlin and Hesse."), never as a number.
> - **The measure:** the control reproduces T3-6 byte for byte; **story classes 8 → 10**; Prussia gives its word for Bavaria against Austria at turn 7 on austerlitz, lodi and ulm; eight world-lines move, each cause named (Saxony is erased on wagram at turn 39); the kept debt stays at 7, T4-2's to clear. The AI-France harness's AI courts keep 7 (8).
> - **Found and fixed on the way:** an alarm guarantee once marched the Porte against France (a guarantee binds against every attacker, so the word is now given only where it binds against the conqueror alone); a quieter world let the league's standing line lead five mornings in ten (a lone filler now yields to the quiet morning); the standing series moved through the write alone (Mack's two more crushed assaults on Lorraine) and is re-pinned with that cause; CLAUDE.md gained the DYNAMISM_SPEC row, paid for by trimming the AI Intent row.
> - **Next:** T4-2 sign at the last (the contract is ruled — `DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §3c G5).

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE CONCERT — T3-6 OCCUPATION IS NOT ANNEXATION — ✅ LANDED November 9, 2026** (row T3-6, ruled by your "make best call, best design not easiest"; `DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §8 row T3-6; the rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §198; the gate amended in `AI_INTENT_SPEC.md` §13.2/§13.3; pins `tests/test_t3_6_the_residual_debt.py`; the seam `a_peace_evacuates_what_it_does_not_cede`, the recorded boards holding it down):
> - **The call:** neither option as offered. March discipline would have armies cross enemy land without taking it — unhistorical, and it bends war score, supply and routing. Redefining the gate alone would hide that AI peaces kept every occupied province forever. So: armies still occupy what they enter, but at a peace between AI courts the winner keeps what the treaty cedes and its authored aims and hands the rest of the loser's homeland back (Pressburg: Vienna evacuated, Venetia and the Tyrol kept). Your own peaces are untouched.
> - **The gate now measures annexation, not occupation:** `annexations_outside_aim` — what a court keeps by right outside its aims at the end. The old capture count stays as a diagnostic of war's footprint.
> - **The measure:** the control reproduces T3-5's chain C; the kept debt falls **42 → 7** ambient, fifteen peaces name what they hand back ("Prussia evacuates Brunswick and Oldenburg"), Saxony survives on austerlitz and Hanover on marengo, **story classes hold at 8**. The AI courts of the AI-France harness keep 8.
> - **The residual is one province:** Franconia, the spoils of Bavaria's elimination on the seven seeds where Austria eats Bavaria by turn 6–12 — no peace ever hands it back. That is Tier 4's own named defect, so Tier 4's done-when now reads it to 0 (Rule 9).
> - **Method, paid for:** a PC15-D4 pin asserting army-held ground always stays was re-seated with its cause (it stays only inside the holder's aims now); the unheld-homeland return runs on the pair road regardless of titles — isolate a new peace rule with a corps standing on the ground (and mind Mack boots at Ulm, in Swabia).
> - **Next:** Tier 4 the Alarm of Europe — its gate in Plan Mode at the tier's head (`DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §3).

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE CONCERT — T3-3 THE LEASH AND ITS CONDUCT RULE — ✅ LANDED November 8, 2026** (§13.2 R4; the conduct half by your "Extend with conduct rule"; `DYNAMISM_SPEC.md` §8 row T3-3; the rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §195; pins `tests/test_t3_3_the_leash.py`; seams `a_leash_pulls_the_march` and `annexation_follows_the_aim`, the recorded boards holding them down):
> - **The census's correction:** AI-3c (the pre-war massing) was ALREADY BUILT at Stage D — your Q1 closed then; §13.1's "remains open" was the July reading, now annotated.
> - **The leash:** `aim_pull_set` — the design's unmet targets ∪ the OUTSTANDING stated object at war (the recapture of lost home soil riding the aim) — consulted by the one covet memo the four ranged pick surfaces read (GR5).
> - **The conduct rule:** an at-war AI court deliberately annexes (P4.5 walk-ins, P4.25 storms) only its pull or an ENEMY'S SEAT. Marches still take undefended ground on entry, so the coalition still marches on Paris — your war keeps its teeth.
> - **Three catches, fixed at the cause:** (1) a war with no record (the legacy world's state-only wars; crafted boards) froze the AI — the rule now binds only where a live war instance stands; (2) R1's capital default — the approved plan's own term, under-implemented at T3-1 — restored PER PAIR (a first cut gave Bavaria London); (3) the four reads SEPARATED — with the seat in the words every coalition court read "Paris" and pushed the PC-F46 peace table below its fold, with it in the pull Austria marched across France instead of for Italy: words and pull read the stated object, the conduct rule adds the seat as a permission, the metric credits it. And the instrument's own catch (the IQ-7 graft control): the boot wars' aims now ride the first row, never the boot block.

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE DYNAMISM PLAN RESTRUCTURED — "THE CONCERT AND THE AWAKENING" — ✅ AUTHORED November 7, 2026, BY YOUR WORD** ("rereturucture plan r making game more dyanmic perhaps we incoporate rising nationalism too and dont make rivals an exact copy of euiv? but soemthing for the era other ideas are good too make this good and have its own idea and implemntation even with its inpiriations"; the deploy cut RULED in Plan Mode — **"After the Questions"**; the owning spec **`docs/DYNAMISM_SPEC.md`**: the gate §0, the thesis §1, the tiers §2, the slices §8; docs only — no code, no measured series moves, Tier 0's routing untouched):
> - **The thesis (the game's own idea, inspirations named honestly):** Europe is a society of courts — conquest spends standing with every third court (THE ALARM, §3), ambition sits at the era's named tables (THE QUESTIONS — German, Italian, Eastern, Polish — §4, deliberately NOT EU4's rival pairs), and nationhood awakens under occupation and humiliation (THE AWAKENING, §5: the ulcer, the liberation levée, the tiger ridden at autonomy ≥ 2 — France exports the force and can ride it or bleed by it). Everything expresses through letters, instruments and the table — never a tooltip number (Q6).
> - **The restructured tiers:** 0 the defects (unchanged; T0-E/F/G next) → 1 the seed → 2 Europe varies + THE CASTING (T2-6: the seed casts an understudy minor from an authored shortlist — the lawful lucky nation) → 3 the strategist → 4 the Alarm + promotion's teeth (a minor that wins may RISE; the Porte's New Order generalized) → 5 the Questions → **S15 THE DEPLOY** → 6 the Awakening (first post-deploy arc) → 7 timing dice only if the story count falls short of 4 → 8 Crowns & Recognition (the marshal king; legitimist recognition; the Congress granting it).
> - **Nearly everything rides existing machinery** (seams verified and named in the spec): `add_threat`'s per-target store, `_derive_weight`'s term table, `_supply_multiplier`, the manpower-regen seam, `AUTONOMY_DRIFT`, the affairs engine, `create_client` already registered at the settlement table.
> - **Gate record §0:** Q1 ruled (the cut); Q2–Q6 standing with defaults, put at each tier's head — the Awakening's per-court grain, the ulcer bleeding France too (default yes: Spain is the point), the four Questions, promotion's teeth at once, alarm never a number.

> **▶ ▶ ▶ THE WHOLE-GAME REVIEW — ✅ LANDED November 6, 2026, BY YOUR WORD ("send out sub agents (sonet) and lets do a review of the games sections … go off"; then "i want the game to be dyanamic"; then "do tiered implementation of all of these make this happen after the bugs fixes") — THE DEPLOY NOW FOLLOWS TIER 0 (THE REVIEW'S DEFECTS) AND THE DYNAMISM TIERS 1–3** (the record `docs/audits/GAME_REVIEW_2026_11_06.md`; the machine record `docs/audits/game_review_2026_11_06_findings.json`; the benchmark archive `docs/audits/score_runs/2026_11_06_ad73ba09/`; 64 digests `docs/audits/playtest_digests/rev1106-*`; the AI-France harness `tools/ai_france_sweep.py`; the rows `BUG_FIXES.md` §GR (63 open at filing) and `DESIGN_REFINEMENT.md` §GRD (36); the plan `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 row S15-R):
> - **The verdict:** sound — no crash, soft-lock, traceback or unexplained gold over about 3,800 played turns (63 driver arms, the benchmark's 36, the AI-France experiment's 30); the benchmark 7.57 over 11 of 14 (October 5: 7.50; command 6.00 → 8.00+ with its P1 cap gone, economy 7 → 8, naval 7.5 → 5.75 on its own SEA arm's staging drift); 98 findings → 85 CONFIRMED / 11 PLAUSIBLE / 2 REFUTED by two refuters each; the one filed P1 (a drilled corps keeps its +20% through every garrison assault, Rule 4) reads P2 by both.
> - **Your four questions:** the economy works at every length played and by its arithmetic beyond (the chest converges on its ceiling; conquest pays steeply and the coalition is the only brake — the crown term's level stays open with you); the enemy AI works for war (the league strikes after it declares: 6–8 of 8 turns; 78% of war turns across eight passive seeds), for the table (a losing court sues, a winning one dictates, every diplomatic verb has a UI control) and for the laws (12–17 by turn 40, no lapse; Prussia and Russia never reach their Staff — the bar is arithmetically out of reach); an AI-run France storms Vienna on turn 5 on nine seeds of ten, destroys Austria's army by turn 6, then marches to Estonia, never writes a letter or enacts a law, and lands in ONE outcome class on nine of ten seeds; the passive Europe is one story on ten of ten (Prussia → Hanover at turns 11–15; Austria eats Bavaria and the Kingdom of Italy; no formation, no volte-face).
> - **Why it is not dynamic, and what will be:** the politics is dice-free by design and meant to vary by the campaign seed — and every live campaign boots `historical` (no seed picker; `/new_game` takes none); each great power has one live design; the military tree has no object once the field army is beaten. The tiers: **Tier 0** the defects (nine slices T0-A … T0-I: the turn's recovery, Rule 4 and the drill, the command road's silences, the books, the sibling sweep of FA-N72, the eclipse brew, the Staff and the Edicts, one re-record chain, the benchmark's own arms) → **Tier 1** the seed rolled and shown at New Game → **Tier 2** the bands widened (the story measure first; peace closes the theatre; Prussia's hook; Russia's two shores and an arbiter that sleeps; Prussia's second design) → **Tier 3** the strategist (the war-aim reader, the leash, the home front, the terms) → **Tier 4** bounded timing dice, gated on Tier 2's story-class count → S15 the deploy. The measure is fixed first: distinct STORY classes across the ten-seed sweep (today 1–2), `captures_outside_aim` (Tier 3's zero), the commanded arms' France band 23–28 as the guard.
> - **Hygiene, disclosed:** a fleet probe ran fifteen end turns through the real command road before sandboxing its save directory, so `saves/autosave.json` (the player's own) was overwritten at 16:51; the named saves are untouched.
> - **Next:** Step 0 ✅ this commit (the record, the rows, the re-route); T0-A the turn's recovery — `docs/NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md`.

ON THE DESK: each letter that waits, "SEALED · ANSWER BY LATE MAY 1806" in rubric with the court's arms or the minister's monogram, the title and the writer, both priced answers, the chip Read the letter ▸ (it closes the page and opens the letter). IN THE OFFING: the Watch's lines, text only. ANSWERED, newest first: the dateline and the title, the apostille — a wax seal and *Send five of the line. — N.* in IM Fell italic — what it did (the receipt), what came of it (the echo dated the next morning, or "At sea — five of the line; word expected about Late February 1806." and later the fate in rubric), the chips from the consequence to its remedy (Bavaria ▸ opens that court's Cabinet page; Admiralty ▸ and Laws ▸ open those books). LEFT UNANSWERED / SET ASIDE: one dim line each, with the price a lapse took. When the Haiti file closes the page prints the closing record and the 1825 line. > - **The design, ✅ ruled (§0b — every ◆ default taken, "Take the defaults"):** **the Haiti letter** — Decrès asks five of the line for Ferrand's garrison at Santo Domingo, on the naval module's own odds (36 in 100 at war, 62 with a window won; the bill of flour and muskets when no hunter is at sea), the Decline priced as the Emperor's own shame in standing where it bites, the record naming the law of 20 May 1802, the expedition, Ferrand's decree, the killings of 1804 and the massacres of 1805, with Haiti's own documents on every arrival (the Constitution's Article 2 three years to the day after the law; the Act of 1804 in Boisrond-Tonnerre's French — two documents alternating by arrival); **the Host's Claims at two corps** (the deck's only reach onto hand-played boards); **a blockade latch** so the letter follows the player's own peace; **the look-ahead** so the first affair is not withdrawn overnight by the Bavarian Match; **the Correspondence**, a page of the Dispatch (no hotkey, no top-bar button, no new endpoint) with the apostille under the seal, the frozen receipt, "what came of it" and chips from the consequence to its remedy; **the letter's dress** — the alpha parchment, ink, the minister's monogram or the court's arms, the record in the old hand, two-line priced buttons, the counsel under them, the seal pressed with the never-played `wax_seal` cue; **the reaction layer** — the echo, a SITUATION line next morning, one Moniteur line, the letter that remembers; **about 10.5 sessions in four shippable tiers** against the October 27 ruling's 5.

> **▶ ▶ ▶ PRE-DEPLOY S14j — THE ECONOMY REBALANCE — ✅ LANDED October 27, 2026** (rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §159; the record `docs/audits/ECONOMY_REBALANCE_2026_10_27.md`; pins `tests/test_s14j_the_economy_rebalance.py`; seams `tests/_oct27_seams.py::S14J_SEAMS_BY_ROW`; the series `tools/_s14j_series_arms.py` → `tools/_s14j_series_arms_final.json`; the row `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S14j)
> - **The ask:** the user — "i mean a revalnce of amounts would fix it, dont be dense, i approve events but lets actually fix this economy before build too it shouldnt run away like this. in reall ife you need to maintain conquests etc etc look into why its a runaway theres ways to fix this". It overrides the golden peace's blessing (August 7), IQ-1 §0.2, SRX-D1 and EAD-6.
> - **Why it ran away (measured):** at peace France paid only for its field army, which melts from ~150–165k to 50–80k while its gross holds at ~4,350, so peace banked +3,000 to +3,800 a turn (75k at T40 on CMD-A, still climbing); nothing France holds cost anything (its garrisons, its conquests at a 10% floor, its clients, its laid-up fleet); even a full army is cheap at peace (the opening balances only on war-time costs); the one brake on a pile, the crown term at 30, drew 1.2% a turn (a plateau of 200–330k, 83 turns away); the courts field one small corps on full income and banked 70–95% of it (all purses 380–453k at T40, the Ottomans 79k).
> - **The rebalance, three amounts, every court:** J1 a corps detached into a garrison keeps its pay (the field rate, in the base); J2 conquered soil costs 0.15 more of its base in every stability tier (0.65/0.50/0.35/0.25 — the uniform shift leaves IGR-E's plunder judge unchanged); J3 the Charges' crown term 30 → 250, and the AI's purse reads the Net before the Charges (else a rich court refused a law or a levy for being rich). The opening is untouched: France's turn-1 bill 2,834, E1 0.722, every court charges 0 at boot.
> - **Taken back on the measure:** paying every capital garrison (330,000 men) drew ~2,200 a turn from the courts — the coalition's war chests emptied and it stopped breaking the conquering France (with that pay held down the scripted conqueror ends at 9–14 provinces; with it, 23–27). Only a detachment is paid.
> - **What it does (crown 250, per seed):** France's chest at T40 18.5k / 31.8k / 26.3k / 23.2k on CMD-H / A / M / REACH-AAR (was 43.0k / 75.4k / 49.7k / 44.9k), steering to ceilings of 16–40k (was 20k–331k); the courts' purses 146–193k at T40 (was 380–453k), the Ottomans 26–27k (was 75–79k); the AI-V arm C median 161k (was 329k), one AI war per seed as before. 250 is the lowest crown that met both targets on all four arms (200 left CMD-A steering to 49k). The checklist like for like: directional 6.75 → 6.88, Economy 7.50 → 8.00, two items flip to pass, none regresses.
> - **The series:** J3 alone moves the ambient board, at [12] — the courts' smaller purses re-time the bribes that buy France's clients (Switzerland 13, was 12; Holland 16, was 17); J1 and J2 held down alone are byte-identical; arm 0 reproduces S14i's series byte for byte. `BASELINE_SERIES` re-pinned (S14i's kept as `SERIES_BEFORE_S14J`, the drill-fix chain linked); the seven-seed probe record re-recorded (crises and wars unchanged on every seed); the DD-1 quick records 0 / 0.
> - **Method traps:** a cost on every court's standing army moves the war balance, not just the purse — measure the conquest road before keeping a court-wide cost; with a high crown the AI's plain post-Charges Net turns negative exactly when a court is rich, so every AI purse test must read before the Charges; a uniform shift of a tier ladder keeps every difference a judge reads.

> **▶ ▶ ▶ RECURRING ERA EVENTS — "AFFAIRS OF STATE" — THE DESIGN GATE — ✅ SPEC AUTHORED and ✅ RULED October 27, 2026 (every recommended default taken, the Edicts in principle)** (the spec `docs/RECURRING_EVENTS_SPEC.md`, its questions §0 Q1–Q14; a side session started from S15's; design only — no game code changed, S15 still NEXT; the row RE after the deploy in `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 and `ROADMAP.md` §"Where the future work lives")
> - **The ask:** the user asked whether the game has recurring events built on blocks of attributes — gold sinks, and brakes on a runaway France. It had none: S14c's four moments are one-shot, and A1 struck the situational class.
> - **The method:** the S14c gate pattern on Sonnet. Three code maps; two web researchers (comparable games; a sourced catalogue of 31 pressures, 1805–1815); a fresh Mode A measure; three designers; three judges (the historian A 44, the architect C 46, the playtester B 41 — each lens its own designer's base); the synthesis; two adversarial critics probing the boot and 22 boards (16 measured, 6 hand-played) — 37 corrections, 8 blocking, every one applied or answered (§18).
> - **What the measure found:** a hoarding France passes 41k by T30 on three of three seeds and buys almost nothing; the scripted conqueror is eclipsed by the coalition on two of three Rhine-road seeds; at the war-time Charges rates (100–450) a gold answer comes back as lower Charges, so gold cannot brake a warring France; the courts hoard 260–329k (EAD-6); human players reach the golden peace by T21, and had enacted laws on four of six roads.
> - **The design:** three recurring letters live on today's boards — the Arch-Chancellor's Letters (the Emperor afield while his standing is spent; authority floored on the grip at 71), the Host's Claims (corps quartered on an ally: gold, or the host's goodwill) and Berthier's Memorial (the Staff at war, where DDB-R7 is silent) — on the moments engine (occurrence ids, the lord in the plan, no new `WorldState` field, the moments blind to the affairs' rows). Ten more wait each on a ruled mechanic: the Edicts first, recommended in principle as the honest recurring gold sink; then the domestic setters, Imperial Governance, a Papal province, EC-5b's leakage, and a court's authority regrowth. Nine first-draft templates fell to the critics' probes (dead premises, dominated forks, map accidents, VP-1's strikes, A1's class).
> - **Ruled the same day:** the user — "Take the recurring-events gate's recommended defaults (docs/RECURRING_EVENTS_SPEC.md §0 Q1–Q14), including the Edicts in principle; RE stays after S15." Row RE after S15: RE-1 and RE-2 after S16, ordered against S17c; RE-3; RE-6, the Edicts, with their contract written at its head and the AI rung's spend of the courts' war chests measured before E1–E3 arm; RE-5's close. About five sessions. A court-run deck and `tempo` are struck by the ruling.
> - **Method traps:** the working copies of STATUS and the plan docs are CRLF while the repository is LF (`core.autocrlf=true`) — a bytes-level edit must keep each file's own endings, and a shell `grep $'\r'` check is not reliable here; a premise "on or beside" a province reads the map's adjacency (Rome borders Piedmont, France's client's province); a floor on raw authority misses the grip the Charges and the aura read; a reserved window around a moment holds only while its premise is stable — make the moments blind to the new rows instead.

> - **The gap, measured first:** on both boards the paymaster had no instrument to bind a member — on the ulm board Austria, spent at 174, broke ranks on exhaustion while Britain paid her 400 a turn, her separate white peace read 7 at the table with nothing in it about the league, and a separate peace of hers broke nothing.
> - **The bond** (`backend/game_logic/bond.py`, the one module): a dated D5 directed record of `kind="bond"` beside sponsorship and neutrality — the payer pays a co-belligerent its own subsidy a turn for 10 turns, and she makes no separate peace with the common enemy while the payer fights on. **The league's paymaster mints it** (`ai_paymaster_binds`, after the exhaustion tick and the war subsidy) for the member that would break ranks now and whom the bond would hold, through the same mint France's Cabinet row calls (GR5).
> - **What it binds:** her break-ranks lines read deeper (−50 → −68, the Pressburg arm −30 → −48, exhaustion 80 → 200 under a 400-gold bond); her row at the table carries "The league's bond" — the stream still owed at the table's own gold rate (−40), only on her separate peace (0 on the league's common peace); the bilateral beside the loyalty term (−12); a separate peace inside the term is `bargain_reneged` against her (the strongest grievance). Measured on the ulm board: Britain binds Austria at turn 18; her separate white peace 7 → −33; her separate peace afterwards is reneged. On the Vienna-held board the paymaster does **not** bind her: at −54 with her design satisfied she is past even the bond's Pressburg line (Pressburg, 1805).
> - **France bids against it — priced, never a veto:** 1,000 gold to her (the bond's table price at the table's own credit) offsets it exactly, or a compensation pays most of it.
